# WeilConjectures.integralDerivedReduction

## Reason

Use the existing scalarReductionDerivedExtension as the body of the integral wrapper.
The two previous constructions apply the same derived-change-of-rings theorem to
identical completed coefficients and quotient projections. Their signatures and
morphisms agree definitionally; the finite-field datum and its characteristic proof
are unused by that construction. Keeping a second nested choice term made adjunction
formulas unfold the completed-ring limit while matching reduction functors.

The module is unclaimed. It gains a public import of
Sheaf.Paper.DerivedScalarExtensionRestrictionAdjunction; import_direction.py confirms
that this introduces no cycle. Every existing signature, proof and other definition
body remains unchanged. The common wrapper introduces no new arbitrary choice. The DAG node
`def-weil-integral-derived-reduction` already attaches this mathematical construction
to `WeilConjectures.scalarReductionDerivedExtension`; its reviewed Lean note explicitly
identifies the two as the same canonical derived tensor. No node attachment changes.

## Old

```lean
noncomputable def integralDerivedReduction :
    completedIntegralDerivedCategory l E Y D hl ⥤
      DerivedCategory (SheafOfModules.{max u (t + 1)} (integralCoefficientSheaf l E Y n)) :=
  (integralReductionDerivedExistence l E Y D hl n).choose
```

## New

```lean
noncomputable def integralDerivedReduction :
    completedIntegralDerivedCategory l E Y D hl ⥤
      DerivedCategory (SheafOfModules.{max u (t + 1)} (integralCoefficientSheaf l E Y n)) :=
  scalarReductionDerivedExtension l E Y n
```

No target statement changes meaning. Existing declaration bodies still prove the
same propositions, and consumers require no source edits. The independent review
should check this definitional identification because target definitions use reduction.
